21:["$","p",null,{"children":["With SQOA Solver by JIJ, you can solve large combinatorial optimization problems on IBM® quantum computers. The solver is based on the Sampling-based Quantum Optimization Algorithm with Quantum Relaxation (SQOA-QR) ",["$","$L1d",null,{"href":"#references","children":"[1]"}],". Unlike quantum algorithms that use a one-to-one mapping between classical decision variables and qubits, SQOA Solver can handle optimization problems with more variables than the number of qubits available on current quantum devices. Its sampling-based design also makes it less sensitive to the physical noise on current quantum hardware. Together, these features make SQOA Solver well suited to optimization problems at this stage of quantum computing."],"className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}]

29:["$","p",null,{"children":["SQOA is a sampling-based framework for combinatorial optimization ",["$","$L1d",null,{"href":"#references","children":"[1]"}],". It combines two techniques: Quantum Random Access Optimization (QRAO) ",["$","$L1d",null,{"href":"#references","children":"[2]"}],", which encodes the problem in a quantum relaxation, and sample-based quantum diagonalization (SQD) ",["$","$L1d",null,{"href":"#references","children":"[3]"}],", also known as quantum-selected configuration interaction (QSCI) ",["$","$L1d",null,{"href":"#references","children":"[4]"}],", which uses the quantum computer only to sample bitstrings and finds the low-energy state on a classical computer. SQOA is a framework in the sense that it fixes this combination but leaves the details to each implementation, such as how the variables are assigned to qubits and which circuits generate the samples."],"className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}]

A classical computer projects the relaxed Hamiltonian onto the subspace spanned by the sampled bitstrings and diagonalizes it to approximate the ground state."],"className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}],"\n",["$","p",null,{"children":"Because the relaxed Hamiltonian is non-diagonal, the diagonalization combines the sampled bitstrings into a superposition. With a diagonal Ising Hamiltonian, every bitstring is already an eigenstate, and the diagonalization would only select the best sample. The same approach has been applied to large-scale problems in quantum chemistry.","className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}],"\n"],"className":"my-12"}]
